### Page 13

employment among young workers in AI-exposed occupations has
weakened, mainly reﬂecting lower hiring rather than more layoﬀs.
According to the Stanford Digital Economy Lab, employment
among 22-25-year-olds in highly exposed occupations was around
19% below where it would have been by June 2026 had it kept
pace with less-exposed occupations. In China, we see similar
pressures on entry-level work reﬂected in rising youth
unemployment. The Chinese government has planned training and

job replacement policies to help alleviate any negative eﬀects of AI
on employment. For developing Asia, particularly India and the
Philippines where outsourcing sectors are highly exposed to AI, the
evidence points to adaptation rather than decline. AI is changing
the nature of outsourced work, not reducing demand for it.
AI is not ﬁring people; it is quietly not hiring them. Still, none of this
points to mass displacement. Yet. And with ageing populations and

persistent labour shortages, the real question may be whether
shortages or AI turn out to be the bigger problem.
For central banks, AI is less of a short-run issue than you might
expect. Higher productivity growth combined with more muted job
growth could be the healthy sign of an economy absorbing a new
technology, and come with more moderate inﬂation. The tricky
part is reading it in real time. A soft payrolls print could signal either
a weakening economy or a more productive one – and the right

policy response would be very diﬀerent. Get it wrong, and a central
bank either cuts rates into a productivity boom or sits on its hands
as the economy weakens.
Who pays?
Is the boom still funded from cash ﬂows, or
are hyperscalers increasingly turning to
debt?
Still mostly from cash ﬂows – but the cushion is thinning and the
borrowing has begun.

### Page 14

The largest hyperscalers have so far paid for the AI build-out out of
their own pockets, which is the biggest diﬀerence from the dotcom
and railway booms. But capex is absorbing a rising share of
operating cash ﬂow, squeezing free cash ﬂow and leaving less room
for share buybacks. For companies that used to return cash to
shareholders at a record pace, that is a notable shift in priorities.
Borrowing is stepping up. Several hyperscalers, such as Amazon,

Alphabet, Meta, and Oracle have issued jumbo bond deals since
2025, with Oracle the most leveraged of the group. Equity markets
are being tapped too: several big tech ﬁrms announced plans to
raise equity in June. None of this is alarming on its own – these are
investment-grade companies with strong balance sheets – but it
marks a change of phase. Cash-funded booms are self-limiting:
they stop when the cash runs out. Debt-funded booms can run
further, and fall harder.

The key is to distinguish between the players. The cash-rich
hyperscalers can absorb a disappointment in AI returns; their core
businesses in advertising, cloud and software keep generating cash
regardless. The neoclouds and AI labs are a diﬀerent story. They
depend entirely on outside money – venture capital, private credit,
vendor ﬁnancing and strategic investors – and their business
models only work if revenue keeps growing spectacularly. They are

the most likely point of stress if ﬁnancing conditions tighten.
In between sits a growing grey zone of leases, joint ventures and
special purpose vehicles that keep debt oﬀ the hyperscalers' own
books.
The direction of travel is clear. The boom started as a self-funded
venture by the richest companies in the world. It is gradually
turning into a credit story.
Leases, SPVs, private credit: how opaque
has AI ﬁnancing become?
More opaque than the hyperscalers' balance sheets suggest – but

so far, the risk rests on some of the strongest borrowers in the
world.

### Page 15

A growing part of AI ﬁnancing is moving oﬀ balance sheet. Special
purpose vehicles and joint ventures, such as Meta's Hyperion data
centre ﬁnanced with Blue Owl, long-term leases and GPU-backed
loans allow companies to fund the build-out without the debt
appearing directly on their own books. Securitisation of data centre
assets through asset-backed securities and commercial mortgage-
backed securities is growing as well, bringing a wider pool of

investors into the game.
Leases are a particular blind spot. They are recognised as on-
balance-sheet commitments only once contracts are ﬁnal; until
then, the extent of incremental lease liabilities is uncertain. There is
also the question of who carries the risk if the tenant walks away
after the initial term. Although initial lease terms of 15 years are
not uncommon, these can still be shorter than the economic life of
the underlying infrastructure, leaving some residual risk around

renewal or re-leasing.
That said, risk has to be assessed contract by contract. The largest
technology ﬁrms, with very solid balance sheets, are well placed to
take on additional liabilities, and much of the oﬀ-balance-sheet
structure ultimately rests on their creditworthiness. The problem is
less hidden insolvency than hidden concentration: it is hard to see
how much of the system leans on the same handful of tenants.
Opacity also complicates the macro picture. When ﬁnancing is

spread across SPVs, private credit funds and securitisations, it
becomes harder for investors, regulators and central banks to see
where risks are concentrated – and harder to judge how a shock
would travel.
Looking ahead, capital may eventually become a major constraint
on the AI build-out, alongside permitting and access to power. The
more the industry relies on creative ﬁnancing, the more it depends
on investors' continued willingness to lend. Financial engineering

can prolong a boom, but it can't make the returns appear.
How exposed are banks, and is private
credit the shadow banking of this cycle?

### Page 16

Direct bank exposure looks modest; private credit is the obvious
weak spot – but contagion would run through markets rather than
bank failures.
Banks' direct exposure to AI looks limited. The hyperscalers fund
themselves mainly through cash ﬂow and bond markets rather
than bank loans. Indirect exposure is harder to gauge: banks lend to
private credit funds, data centre developers and construction ﬁrms.
Private credit is the obvious weak spot. Is it the shadow banking of

this cycle? Partly. It has less of the maturity mismatch that made
pre-2008 shadow banking so fragile: much of the capital is locked
up in closed-end funds, so investors can't run. But it shares other
features: opacity, infrequent valuation marks and leverage on
leverage, with funds borrowing from banks to lend to borrowers
that are themselves highly leveraged. And it is increasingly exposed
to AI through loans to neoclouds, GPU-backed ﬁnancing and data

centre developers.
The pressure point is the semi-liquid vehicles sold to retail and
wealth clients. They promise periodic liquidity on assets that are
anything but liquid. In a downturn, redemption requests could force
managers to impose gates, damaging conﬁdence across the asset
class. So far, these vehicles are not core to the AI build-out, but they
are where a shock could turn into a story.
If stress does materialise, we would expect contagion to run

through tighter ﬁnancial conditions and wider credit spreads across
the economy rather than through bank failures. That is less
dramatic than 2008, but not harmless: a repricing of credit risk
would raise funding costs far beyond tech, hitting the very non-tech
investment that is already struggling.
Banks may well escape an AI bust largely unscathed. The economy
they lend to may not.
Vendor ﬁnancing déjà vu: should we worry
when chipmakers fund their own

customers?

### Page 17

Worth watching, but this is not Lucent and Nortel all over again –
mainly because the balance sheets are much stronger.
The dotcom precedent is uncomfortable. Lucent, Nortel and Cisco
lent money to telecom start-ups so they could buy their
equipment. When the customers went bust, the suppliers took the
hit, and much of the reported demand turned out to have been
ﬁnanced by the sellers themselves.
Today, there are echoes. Chipmakers are taking stakes in AI labs

and neoclouds that then buy their chips. The same handful of ﬁrms
raise money, buy chips, lease compute and book revenue oﬀ one
another. The concern is less solvency than signal: circular deals
inﬂate reported demand and blur true end-demand. Revenue that
only exists because the supplier paid for it isn't demand.
But the picture diﬀers in important ways. Many of these contracts
appear to be normal commercial arrangements, and AI labs also
generate fast-growing revenues from customers outside these

related deals. AI-related revenues are still only part of the much
larger businesses of the hyperscalers. And the macro backdrop is
very diﬀerent: the overall US corporate debt-to-GDP ratio is not
worrisome, in stark contrast to the leverage that built up before the
dotcom bust and the global ﬁnancial crisis. Circularity in a cash-rich
system is a transparency problem; circularity in a leveraged system
is a solvency problem.
That does not mean investors can relax. Circular deals create

concentration risk: if one key AI lab stumbles, the shock would
travel quickly through its suppliers, landlords and investors, who are
often the same companies. And they make it harder to judge how
much genuine end-demand there is for AI – precisely the question
on which the whole boom rests.
The test is simple: follow the money until it reaches a customer
who isn't also an investor. The longer that trail gets, the more
nervous we should become.
Higher Fed rates helped end the dotcom

bubble. Could monetary policy or bond

### Page 18

yields pop this one too?
Probably not as the trigger – but monetary policy could make any
bust more painful, because the safety net is thinner than in 2001.
In 1999-2000, the Fed hiked rates to 6.5% before the dotcom
bubble burst, then cut by almost ﬁve percentage points in 2001.
This time, an energy shock is keeping inﬂation above target. Actual
rate hikes and more rate hikes to come could bring both the Federal
Reserve and European Central Bank closer to risky territory.

High policy rates do not necessarily stop AI investment ﬁrst. If
expected returns on AI remain very high, capital may keep ﬂowing
in while ﬁnancing becomes uneconomic for lower-return
investment elsewhere. Tight money could deepen the split between
tech and non-tech investment rather than end the boom.
At the moment, we expect hyperscaler funding needs to increase
further as investment in AI infrastructure, data centres and power
capacity accelerates. We forecast total issuance of around

US$280bn in 2027, including approximately US$190bn in USD and
US$65bn in EUR (€60bn). For European credit markets, we estimate
a net TMT supply number of €103bn, which includes US$65bn
coming from hyperscalers.
The long end of the yield curve matters more than the policy rate.
As the AI build-out becomes increasingly debt-funded, higher bond
yields and term premia raise the cost of ﬁnancing long-term
investments, from data centres to power plants. A sustained rise in

long-term yields would therefore hit hardest where the build-out
depends most on external funding.
The bigger issue is the safety net. If the bubble bursts, central banks
can't come to the rescue as aggressively as in 2001 while inﬂation
is above target. Fiscal space is also far more limited than a quarter
of a century ago.
But don't rule out a diﬀerent kind of rescue. AI is not just an
economic tool; it is also about power – geopolitical and
technological. If things went south for the sector, some form of

government support would be conceivable, from public

procurement to guarantees or targeted support for strategically

Monetary policy is unlikely to pop this bubble. But it could decide
how hard the landing is – and Washington may decide who gets a
parachute.
Bubble or not?
What do valuations and revenue gaps tell
us?
Bubble-like pockets, but not a bubble across the board: the leaders
are hugely proﬁtable, while the real uncertainty sits with the AI

labs and the revenue gap.
Valuations ﬁrst. At its 2000 peak, Cisco traded at well over 100
times earnings. Today’s AI leaders are expensive, but proﬁtable,
and analysts expect earnings growth to bring their multiples down
considerably. Microsoft’s P/E is expected to fall from 29.2x based on
ﬁscal 2026 earnings to 21.9x in 2028, Amazon’s from 28x based on
2025 earnings to 21x in 2027, and Alphabet’s from 20.9x to 17.9x
over the same period. With earnings estimates being higher than a

couple of months ago, that does not look particularly stretched.
The larger uncertainty sits with the AI labs, whose valuations follow
the venture capital playbook: spectacular revenue growth,
uncertain long-term economics.
Then the revenue gap. Capex is running ahead of AI revenues, as it
can take several years for investment to translate fully into
revenues. Amazon, Microsoft and Alphabet have invested around
US$600bn over 2023-25. As a rough rule of thumb, every dollar of

AI infrastructure may need to generate 40-50 cents of annual
revenue (assuming hyperscaler-like margins), implying US$240-
300bn a year – although not all of this capex was AI-related. That
looks large, but less so next to labour costs. At US$100,000 per
employee, US$300bn is the cost of three million workers. AI doesn't

### Page 20

need to replace them: a 20% productivity gain across 15 million
employees would deliver the same value. Consumers alone won't
close the gap; the money has to come from enterprises.
Two caveats. Concentration: the weight of the largest tech stocks in
the S&P 500 exceeds the dotcom peak, so any disappointment
would hit the whole index hard. And circularity in ﬁnancing and
revenues makes true end-demand harder to measure.
Finally, a bubble in prices doesn't mean the technology is a dud.

Railways had a bubble. So did the internet. Both still changed the
world.
What could end the boom prematurely:
disappointing demand, power bottlenecks, a
cheaper-model breakthrough, or Taiwan?
Most likely not a single trigger, but a slow squeeze: overcapacity,
eroding margins and tighter ﬁnancing if the revenue gap fails to
close.
Start with the usual suspects. Demand could disappoint if
enterprise adoption stalls and pilots fail to turn into proﬁts. Power

bottlenecks are more likely to cap the pace of the boom than to end
it, and could even stretch it out. A Taiwan crisis remains a low-
probability, extreme-impact event.
Cheaper models are the most intriguing risk. The DeepSeek shock of
January 2025 wiped almost US$600bn oﬀ Nvidia's market value in
a single day. Cheaper compute can raise demand – the Jevons
paradox – but higher volumes can come with lower margins. That
makes the proﬁtability of AI labs such as OpenAI and Anthropic the

big question mark. If it disappoints, the hardware will still be
needed, but the build-out would likely slow. Nvidia's exceptionally
high margins could also normalise if competition increases or the
scarcity value of leading-edge compute declines.
Missing from most lists is ﬁnancing: rising bond yields, a credit
event at a leveraged neocloud or private credit fund, or an energy
shock that keeps central banks hawkish.

### Page 21

Ultimately, we see the biggest risk in overcapacity. Continued
innovation in chips, more eﬃcient models and optimised
algorithms could eventually create an imbalance between supply
and demand, pushing down utilisation and returns. For now,
though, the industry is still struggling to scale compute and secure
enough power, while new business models and applications keep
emerging.
There is one more way to read today's capacity commitments: as
strategic entry deterrence. Announcing gigantic investment plans

discourages competitors from entering the market – and some of
those plans may never be fully executed if returns disappoint. That
would be a quieter ending than a crash. Not with a bang, but with a
series of downward revisions to capex guidance.
If tech stocks fall 30%, how much does the
wealth effect hit US consumption?
Less than you might think, but more than during the dotcom bust –
and the real damage would come through investment and jobs.
US equity wealth is highly concentrated. The top 20% of

households by income (earning more than $155k a year) hold
84.1% of equities, mutual funds and deﬁned-contribution pension
assets. That limits the wealth eﬀect. Fed research suggests the
marginal propensity to consume out of wealth fell from around 3.5
cents per dollar (1964-2011) to 2.6 cents in 2020.
A 30% stock market correction would wipe out roughly $24.5tn of
household wealth. At 2.6 cents per dollar, that means a drop in
consumer spending of around $640bn, or 2.9% of nominal

consumer spending (an annualised $22.3tn in August). With wealth
even more concentrated than in 2020, the multiplier is probably
weaker; at 2.2 cents, the hit would be around 2.4%. That is a level
eﬀect, typically playing out over one to two years – but a big
number nonetheless.
History oﬀers some comfort: during the dotcom bust, US consumer
spending barely ﬂinched. But it could be worse this time. First,
wealthy households are currently the only ones holding up

### Page 22

spending; the bottom 60%, who spend roughly as many dollars as
the top 20%, are squeezed by tariﬀs and higher fuel prices. Second,
the Fed has less room to cut than in 2001. Third, the credit cushion
is thinner: rising delinquencies on car loans and credit cards show
stress building at lower incomes.
The good news: this is not 2008. US household debt stands at
around 70% of GDP, the same as in 2001 and well below the 100%
reached in 2007.
In the end, the wealth eﬀect is the smaller channel. After the

dotcom bust, business investment fell by 11% in two years. IT
equipment was the least of it, falling just 6%, transport equipment
fell 21% and manufacturing buildings plunged by a whopping
55%. Tech may light the fuse, but the explosion happens elsewhere.
What would a tech sell-off mean for the
dollar and FX markets?
Not necessarily the classic safe-haven dollar rally – this time, a
tech sell-oﬀ could well hurt the dollar.
The usual playbook says risk-oﬀ means dollar up. But an AI-led

correction would be a US-centric shock. Foreign investors hold large,
partly unhedged positions in US equities, built up over a decade of
American outperformance. A sell-oﬀ in US tech could trigger
repatriation and higher hedge ratios, both dollar-negative – as in
the 'sell America' episode of April 2025. The IMF estimates the
dollar would fall by around 6% in an AI-led equity correction.
The rates channel points in the same direction. Negative wealth

eﬀects, weaker consumption and slower US growth would
eventually push the Fed towards rate cuts, narrowing interest rate
diﬀerentials against the dollar. The timing would depend on
inﬂation: with prices still running above target, the Fed may cut
later than markets hope, which could limit the dollar's fall in the
early stages.
Among the winners, the yen and the Swiss franc would play their
traditional safe-haven role. The euro could beneﬁt relatively, given

Europe's lower exposure to both AI capex and equity wealth (4Q). A

### Page 23

tech crash made in America would, for once, be a reason to hold
European assets rather than ﬂee them.
The losers would be the currencies of Asian tech exporters, notably
the Taiwan dollar and the Korean won, whose economies are
leveraged bets on AI demand. They would come under heavy
pressure, although intervention by local authorities and the legacy
of huge trade surpluses would cushion the blow somewhat.
The bottom line: in an AI bust, the dollar may lose its reputation as

the place to hide. Not because the world has found a better safe
haven, but because this time the ﬁre would start in the safe house.
Our base case for 2027: boom, plateau or
bust, and which signposts should readers
watch?
Our base case is a plateau at altitude: the boom continues in 2027,
but its contribution to growth peaks. A bust remains the main risk
scenario.
Early indicators point to tight semiconductor supply into 2028,
suggesting the investment cycle still has legs. But as capex growth

slows, AI's contribution to US growth should fade.
The diﬀerence between the two scenarios matters enormously for
the US. A plateau would give the non-tech economy room to
breathe: capital, energy and workers no longer absorbed by AI
could ﬂow back, allowing non-tech investment – and potentially
employment – to rebound. A bust would be a very diﬀerent animal:
an abrupt choking oﬀ of tech investment, similar to 2001,
combined with a negative wealth eﬀect on consumers through

falling stock markets. And this time, central banks would have less
room for rate cuts as inﬂation remains their main concern.
Which signposts should readers watch? For the boom itself, the key
indicators are hyperscaler capex guidance, Taiwanese order books
and lead times, and AI revenues relative to capex. Markets will focus
on realised AI revenues at the large technology ﬁrms, margins on
incremental revenue and how the next phase of the build-out will

be funded; ﬁgures that disappoint relative to company budgets
and analysts' forecasts could trigger a sell-oﬀ.
On ﬁnancing, credit spreads on AI-related debt, private credit ﬂows

and redemptions, and bond yields and term premia will show
whether investors remain willing to fund the build-out. In the real
economy, the questions are whether non-tech investment ﬁnally
recovers, how hiring in AI-exposed sectors develops, and what
productivity, grid connection and power data are telling us.
The question for 2027 isn't whether AI will change the world. It's
whether anyone gets paid for it in time. If earnings fail to match
sky-high expectations, the plateau could turn out to be a ledge.

3 November 2026
• Average consumer spend across 
the Westpac-DataX Consumer 
Panel remained resilient, rising 
1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr. 
• While some of this reflected a price-
driven surge in fuel spend (+0.3%qtr) 
discretionary categories also posted 
robust gains (contributing 0.9ppts 
to the headline rise) with non-fuel 
essential categories contributing 
positively as well (+0.5ptps).
• Households continued to add to savings 
and buffers. Savings rose 1.3%qtr, led 
by younger households and mortgagors, 
with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments. 
• The detail shows financial pressures 
remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

## Document 377: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.

New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM) (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note, 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 October that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available September data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5-2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five-year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI- and semiconductor-
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housing, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private-sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5 bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong 
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflation risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weakening 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa-City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI-electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re-export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger demand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low-income 
households. Despite the quarterly 
moderation, we maintain our full-year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass-through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

景氣穩健但不過熱，美股短線有
壓然無礙趨勢
分析師／張志揚

美洲投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/30
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《盤勢看法》
科技類股於10月中旬出現明顯修正，不過市場資金
並未明顯撤離，而是在其他類股輪動，使標普500
指數的修正幅度相對有限。長期來說，穩健的經濟
基本面才是股市行情延續的關鍵，而目前的景氣和
企業獲利支持這樣的趨勢，只是高利率環境仍將使
市場資金更加嚴格看待資產的成長性。拉美方面，
現任左翼總統魯拉在10月初第一輪投票落後的情況
下，於第二輪決選中逆轉，市場重新定價財政前景，
利率上升壓力將提高。據此，將拉美和巴西股市的
評等由正向下調為中立。

《貨幣政策-聯準會偏鷹發言但不急於連續升息》
10月FOMC會議如市場預期維持政策利率在3.75%-
4.0%不變，本次會議的重點可歸納如下：
◼ 聯準會認為美國經濟非常強勁，消費支出健康，
企業投資強勁，通膨仍處於偏高水準，「但也
不急於立刻升息」。華許的解釋並不是通膨風
險已經消失，而是認為先前的政策行動已經透
過金融條件產生緊縮效果，投資人開始更加審
慎地配置資金，部分資產的信用利差擴大。
◼ 縮減資產負債表不是當前政策的優先選項，相
關問題仍交由工作小組研究，這表示雖然他在
長期制度設計上支持重新檢討聯準會資產負債
表的規模與功能，也不代表短期內就會立即推
動更激進的量化緊縮。
雖然華許一如既往地不提供對未來利率路徑的承諾，

但從記者會的發言可大致推論，目前的方案是間隔
一次會議再採取下一個新的行動，本次會議暫停升
息，12月會議將進行下一次升息。2027年1月的會
議按兵不動，3月會議視市場情況再決定。本行的基
準情境是，2027年開始會看到關稅效應、能源衝擊
對通膨的影響逐漸消退，因此不認為2027年3月有
升息的必要性。風險則在於美伊衝突進一步升級，
使企業利用年初例行漲價的機會將成本壓力轉嫁給
消費者，若此，3月可能再升息一次。但本行仍定調
9月以來的升息決策為「風險管理式的升息」，而非
持續性的升息循環。加上華許並未將縮減資產負債
表列入優先事項，過度緊縮的風險不大。
重點摘要
• 本行仍定調9月以來的升息決
策為「風險管理式的升息」，
而非持續性的升息循環。加
上華許並未將縮減資產負債
表列入優先事項，過度緊縮
的風險不大

《總經情勢和殖利率-景氣穩健但不到過熱程度，美
債壓力有緩和跡象》
美國3Q26 GDP季增年率3.4%，高於2Q26的2.2%，
主要受惠於消費表現優於預期。在油價高漲、利率
上揚的環境當中，美國消費的強勁程度相當出乎意
料。其實個人所得成長率、就業動能，都僅是溫和
成長，整體消費的動能，和股市帶來的財富效應息
息相關。數據顯示，美國家庭持有的股權價值已接
近年稅後所得的3倍。但這也帶來一些疑慮，如k型
消費、景氣對股市的敏感度太高等。綜合來看，美
國的經濟表現不錯，但並不到過熱程度。
美國10年期公債殖利率自7月開始一波上行趨勢，
10月上旬一度升破5.3%，但近期已回落到5%之下，
一部分原因是市場對貨幣政策的共識已經開始凝聚，

以一季進行一次利率調整為原則，目前的經濟數據
尚不足以改變這點；另外原因是如同華許所述，長
率上升、金融條件往收緊的方向，已經透過融資成
本、信用利差、投資人風險偏好來影響資本配置。
當然這並不意味著公債利率將開啟下行趨勢，地緣
風險和財政問題仍不時帶來干擾，不過至少就近期
走勢而言，美債市場面臨的壓力已有初步緩和跡象。
重點摘要
• 美國的經濟表現不錯，但並
不到過熱程度。市場對貨幣
政策的共識已經開始凝聚，
以一季進行一次利率調整為
原則，美債市場面臨的壓力
有初步緩和跡象
2
資料來源: BCA，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

各輪景氣循環中，資產價值增幅占可支配所得的比例

《美股盤勢-景氣穩健但不過熱，美股短線有壓然無
礙趨勢》
科技類股於10月中旬出現明顯修正，尤其半導體相
關類股面臨較大的估值壓力，不過市場資金並未明
顯撤離，而是在其他類股輪動，使標普500指數的
修正幅度相對有限。
本行先前提及「美股通常會在升息初期承壓，但若
非如同2022年般的連續性升息，股市的趨勢不至於
受到影響」，此定調仍延續。雖然市場不時會處於
「壞消息才是好消息」的情緒當中，看到表現不錯
的經濟數據反而造成市場的震盪修正，但長期來說，
穩健的經濟基本面才是股市行情延續的關鍵，而目
前的景氣和企業獲利支持這樣的趨勢，只是高利率
環境仍將使市場資金更加嚴格看待資產的成長性，
具有強健資產負債表使其較不易受到融資成本上升

影響、具防禦性或差異化成長動能的企業，將能維
持相對韌性。
重點摘要

《拉美股市-巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選，市場重新定價
財政前景，股市評等下調為中立》
巴西總統大選出現戲劇性逆轉，現任左翼總統魯拉
在10月初第一輪投票落後的情況下，於第二輪決選
中逆轉，擊敗右翼對手小波索納洛，這是1989年恢
復總統直選以來首次出現第二輪決選逆轉，巴西股
債匯市均出現劇烈下跌。
並不能說魯拉當選對巴西的經濟不利，他的前次當
選也並未引起股市震盪。巴西股市在估值的吸引力、
礦產的發展，都仍具有長線優勢。然目前資本市場
確實較青睞較為親美和親商的波索納洛，巴西股市8
月中以來的漲幅不小，很大程度是反映波索納洛取
得優勢，因此市場需要修正先前的定價，外資撤出。
另外，魯拉第一輪投票後為挽救選情，承諾大量財
政政策，如擴大所得稅扣除額、提高家庭補助金等，

在全球主要國家多面臨財政風險之際，市場對財政
前景的檢視力道將加大，利率上升壓力將提高。據
此，將拉美股市和巴西股市的評等由正向下調為中
立。
重點摘要
• 巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選，市
場重新定價財政前景，利率
上升壓力將提高。據此，將
拉美股市和巴西股市的評等
由正向下調為中立。

《通訊服務龍頭短期營運雜音較多》
臉書 FY26Q3 財報營收、獲利皆優於預期。管理層
對 AI 投資變現的論述較上季更具體。AI 對廣告價
量的優化仍提供營收成長上行空間，但 Muse 規模
擴張與變現路徑短期變數仍多。谷歌同季財報反映
谷歌業務雙線推展的營運壓力。谷歌雲年增雖令人
驚豔，但搜尋業務營收出於預期的減速引發對做為
現金牛的搜尋業務競爭力的擔憂。隨著 AI Mode 相
關廣告業務全球推展、TPU 系統銷售爬坡與第三方
算力橋接陸續開始，谷歌短期雜音漸增。本行認為
兩龍頭短期上行動能較有限。但另一方面，本行認

為估值已反映大部分的逆風，與歷史估值區間對比
向下修正空間有限。因此，看法仍維持中立。

《臉書財報優於預期，谷歌財報較為錯綜…》
臉書 (META) FY26Q3 財報營收略優於預期、調整後
EPS 優於預期。但積極的資本支出擴張對獲利能力
的影響仍顯著。管理層指引顯示營收成長持續降溫。
自由現金流轉負，但資本支出低於市場預期。管理

層對 AI 投資變現的論述更聚焦在 Muse 為起點的規
模擴張帶動的商家交易與價值鏈環節滲透，較上季
更具體。在資本支出預期仍將持續，以在 FY2026-
27 最大化算力產能下，單位模型運算成本的下行曲
線將決定規模擴張的速度，值得持續留意模型迭代
與特化背後的成本下行發展，與商家接入臉書生態
系的速度。
谷歌 (GOOGL) 26Q3 財報反映谷歌業務雙線推展的
營運壓力。總營收雖約略符合共識預期，但營收組
成與預期有顯著差異。谷歌雲年增顯著高於預期，
但搜尋業務營收明顯減速且低於預期引發市場擔憂
搜尋業務競爭力。每股盈餘顯著低於預期，雖然 6-
7 成係因 SpaceX 持股的未實現評價損失，但因 AI
投資、TPU 系統銷售與搜尋成長減速導致利潤率也
大出市場預期之外。隨著 AI Mode 相關廣告業務全

開始，市場將更加聚焦檢視雙線的業務成長與獲利
能力動態。

## Document 520: 中小企業向け補助金政策・成長支援制度の課題 〜賃上げ要件の意義、成長宣言制度の効果、金利上昇下での財務・金融機関連携～

Source PDF: all report pdf/中小企業向け補助金政策・成長支援制度の課題 〜賃上げ要件の意義、成長宣言制度の効果、金利上昇下での財務・金融機関連携～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年 11月 24日

1/5 
中小企業向け補助金政策・成長支援制度の課題 
〜賃上げ要件の意義、成長宣言制度の効果、金利上昇下での財務・金融機関連携～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト 永濱 利廣 
（℡：050-5474-7492）

（要旨） 
⚫ 補助金と賃上げ要件については、生産性向上と賃金上昇の好循環や人手不足下での人材流出防止
を狙う一方、大型支援の未達時返還リスクは財務の脆弱な企業にとって重荷となり、挑戦とリス
クの二面性を持つ。 
⚫ 成長宣言制度と政策転換については、「100億円宣言」や2027年度からの「10億円企業宣言」、
ポータル公開は経営者の意識変革や対外シグナルとして機能する一方、政策は成長意欲の高い企
業への重点支援へ傾きつつあり、成長投資が難しい企業には事業承継や M&A、再構築などの個別
支援が必要。 
⚫ 投資判断・資金繰り・金融機関の関与については、制度ごとの違いに振り回されず本来の事業採
算性を見極めることが重要であり、後払い方式や金利上昇局面では立替負担や返還リスクを踏ま
えた厳格なキャッシュフロー管理が求められる。また、地銀や信金の関与は事業計画の信頼性向
上と伴走支援・融資円滑化に資する。 
⚫ 政策の転換点において、企業は自社の財務体力と事業性を踏まえた自律的な判断を行い、地域金
融機関の伴走支援やセーフティネットと組み合わせて持続的成長につなげることが鍵となる。

１．はじめに 
現在の中小企業政策は、従来の「広く薄く支える保護型」から、賃上げと生産性向上を伴う「成長意
欲の高い企業への重点支援型」へと大きく舵を切っている。 
そこで本稿では、設備投資補助金に組み込まれた賃上げ要件の経済的狙い、大型支援に伴う返還リス
クの評価、「100億円宣言」や「10億円企業宣言」が経営行動に与える影響、さらには金利上昇局面にお
ける資金繰り管理や地域金融機関の役割について考察する。

２.補助金政策における賃上げ要件と投資リスクの評価 
まずは、設備投資への補助金に賃上げ要件を設ける経済的狙いから見ていこう。省力化投資補助金
（一般型における1人当たり給与支給総額の年平均成長率 3.5％以上、カタログ注文型における給与総
額 6％以上の増加等で補助上限の引き上げ）や中小企業成長加速化補助金（1人当たり給与支給総額の

### Page 2

2/5 
年平均成長率4.5％以上）など、近年の主要補助金には厳格な賃上げ要件が組み込まれている。そし
て、この政策設計には以下の二つの経済的狙いがある。 
一つ目が、生産性と賃上げの好循環の強化である。というのも、単なる機械設備の更新による省力
化にとどまらず、創出した余力や向上した生産性を従業員の賃金上昇（労働分配率の適正化）へ確実
に還元させることで、マクロ経済全体のデフレマインドを払拭する意図がある。 
そして二つ目が、賃金上昇を伴う成長モデルへの転換である。こちらは、構造的な人手不足が進行
する中、賃上げを伴わない省力化投資は結果として労働力の外部流出を招く。付加価値額の拡大を通
じた持続的な賃上げを政策的に誘導し、企業の生存基盤そのものを強化するインセンティブ設計とな
っている。 
続いて、中小企業成長加速化補助金における「挑戦」と「リスク」の二面性についても見ていこ
う。最大 5億円という大型支援枠を用意するこの補助金は、基準率 4.5％などの賃上げ要件を未達の場
合に補助金返還の可能性を伴う。よって、この制度は中小企業にとって「挑戦の後押し」と「財務リ
スク」の双方を内包している。 
具体的には、まずハイリスク・ハイリターンのトレードオフである。というのも、大型の設備投資
や事業再構築に挑む中堅・中小企業にとって強力な推進力となる一方、景気後退や原材料高騰など予
期せぬ外部環境の変化によって賃上げ目標を下回った場合、返還義務は財務基盤の脆弱な企業にとっ
て致命的な打撃となり得るからである。 
また、緊張感の醸成と応募手控えのジレンマもある。というのも、経営計画の精度や実行力を高め
る「規律」として機能する半面、過度なリスク回避心理を生み、本来支援を必要とする意欲ある企業
の挑戦を委縮させる側面も否定できないからである。

図表１ 補助金政策における賃上げ要件と投資リスクの評価

３．「成長宣言制度」による経営行動の変革と政策転換 
 次に、「100億円宣言」が企業の成長意欲と経営行動に与える影響についても見ていこう。こちらに
ついては、売上 10億円規模の企業が「実現できるとは思わないが、とにかく宣言した」という姿勢で
「100億円宣言」に参画するケースも見られる。一見すると形式的な参加に映るが、制度の趣旨に照ら
せば一定の合理性と行動変容の契機を有している。 
というのも、まずはコミットメントとアンカリング効果が指摘できる。具体的には、あえて高い目
標を対外的に宣言することで、経営者自身や組織の思考枠組み（メンタルモデル）を大きく引き上
げ、従来の延長線上ではない大胆な設備投資や人材採用に踏み切るトリガーとなる。 
また、形骸化リスクと経営刷新の強制力も期待できる。というのも、単なる「お題目」に終わる懸
念はあるものの、宣言を起点として社内体制の整備やビジネスモデルの再構築に着手せざるを得ない
状況を作り出す点に制度的な意義があるからである。 
続いて、中小企業政策の転換と、成長投資が難しい企業への支援のあり方についても見ていこう。
こちらは、採用や企業ブランドにも活用できる「100億円宣言」のロゴマーク付与などに象徴されるよ
うに、政府は限られた財政リソースの中で日本経済全体の生産性を引き上げるため、現状維持にとど
制度・政策テーマ 主な要件・制度設計 政策的な狙いと経営上のインパクト

賃上げ基準率：1人当た
り給与支給総額 4.5％等
（未達時返還） 
非連続的な事業拡大を強力に支援す
る一方、計画未達時の返還リスクに
より経営陣に高い実行責任を課す。 
100億円宣言（既実施） / 
10億円企業宣言（2027年度
〜）

ル公開

信金の計画関与

公開による規律付け、および地域金
融機関の伴走支援を通じた成長気運
の波及。

### Page 4

4/5 
まる企業を広く支える従来型政策から、成長意欲の高い企業を重点的に後押しする「選択と集中」へ
と転換を進めている。 
一方で、人手不足や後継者難から事業継続そのものに悩む企業に対しては、成長投資とは異なるア
プローチが不可欠である。具体的には、第三者承継を含む事業承継支援、M&Aによる経営統合、あるい
は円滑な事業再構築や秩序ある廃業支援など、個社の実情に即したきめ細やかなセーフティネットの
整備が求められる。 
こうした中、2027年度には「10億円企業」宣言制度が創設される。こちらは、より規模の小さい企
業群に成長志向を浸透させる役割を担う。小規模事業者が「次のステージへ成長する」というマイン
ドセットを持つことで、地域経済やサプライチェーン全体の底上げが期待される。ローカル・エコノ
ミーの核となる中核企業の裾野が広がることで、地域全体の雇用魅力度や賃金水準が引き上げられる
波及効果が見込まれる。 
なお「10億円宣言」においては、各社の成長目標や具体的な取り組みをポータルサイトで公開する
仕組みになっており、こちらは経営上の透明性と規律を高める二つの重要な機能があるとされてい
る。まずは、ピアプレッシャー（同調圧力）による実行力の担保である。というのも、固有の目標と
計画をあえてオープンにすることで、競合他社への対抗意識や適度な社会的プレッシャーを生み出
し、計画倒れを防ぐ抑止力として機能するということである。また、ステークホルダーへの信頼シグ
ナルとして、取引先、求職者、金融機関に対して自社の明確な成長シナリオを提示することで、優秀
な人材の獲得や新たな業務提携、オープンイノベーションの構築を有利に進めるシグナル効果を発揮
することが期待される。

４．企業の投資判断・資金繰り管理と地域金融機関の役割 
以上の制度の違いを踏まえて、補助金選択と投資判断の注意点についてみていこう。まず、補助金
ごとに賃上げ要件や補助率、対象経費が異なる現状では、企業の投資判断が制度の枠組みに左右され
るリスクがある。本来行うべき事業投資の本質よりも、「どの補助金の要件に合わせやすいか」とい
う基準で投資のタイミングや内容が歪められる（補助金ありきの投資）事態は避けなければならな
い。企業は自社の財務体力、中長期のキャッシュフロー予測、そして要件未達時の返還リスクを総合
的に勘案し、事業の採算性を最優先に判断すべきである。 
また、金利上昇局面における資金繰り管理の留意点としては、補助率 2分の1の制度であっても残
りの半分は自己資金または借入で賄う必要があり、さらに補助金は原則「後払い」であるため、投資
実行時点では消費税分を含めた全額を一時的に立て替えなければならない。特に、金利が上昇する局

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
面では、つなぎ融資や設備資金の借入コストが増大するだけでなく、万が一の業績悪化や賃上げ未達
によって補助金返還が発生した場合、利払い負担と返還債務が重なり企業の財務を急速に圧迫するリ
スクがある。金利上昇リスクを織り込んだ上で、返済猶予期間の確保や手元流動性に十分な余裕を持
たせた資金計画の策定が不可欠である。 
なお、「10億宣言」においては地方銀行や信用金庫が事業計画づくりに関与する設計となってお
り、この背景には、金融機関の「目利き力」と「伴走支援機能」を政策に組み込む狙いがある。とい
うのも、金融機関が計画策定の初期段階から関与することで、単なる資金供給にとどまらず、事業性
評価に基づいた実効性の高い経営改善・成長戦略の立案が促される。現場において宣言や補助金採択
が金融機関から高く評価され融資が受けやすくなるのは、金融機関自身の事前関与によって事業計画
の蓋然性と信頼性が担保され、リスクテイキングした融資判断を行いやすくなるという政策的意図が
有効に機能しているためである。

５．おわりに 
以上のように、現在の中小企業政策は、単なる設備導入への資金補填から、賃上げと付加価値向上
を伴う「自律的な成長企業」の育成へと大きく軸足を移している。各種補助金における厳格な賃上げ
要件や未達時の返還規定、「100億円宣言」や「10億円企業宣言」における計画の公表は、いずれも
経営陣に対して高い目標設定と計画実行の規律を求める仕組みである。 
一方で、金利上昇や人手不足といった厳しい経営環境下においては、補助金ありきの安易な投資は
かえって企業の財務体力を損なうリスクを孕む。中小企業には、自社の事業戦略とキャッシュフロー
の実態に即した冷静な投資判断が求められる。同時に、地域金融機関による伴走支援と、成長投資が
困難な企業に対する事業承継・再構築支援を両輪で機能させることが、日本経済全体の持続的な底上
げにつながる鍵となろう。

## Document 585: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_6533 晶心科_許芝瑄_20261113

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_6533 晶心科_許芝瑄_20261113.pdf

### Page 1

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R   1

法說重點摘要

晶心科 
(6533 TT, NT$249.5, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 晶心科 3Q26 營收季減 24.6%，低於市場共識，主因 2Q26 認列北
美 CSP 大客戶授權案導致基期較高。公司於 2Q26 已將人力精簡至
320 人，使 3Q26 營業費用季減 34.1%，營業損失收斂至 0.1 億
元，每股虧損 0.58 元。展望 4Q26，公司預估 EPS 將轉正，預期費
用將維持 3Q26 水準，並目標未來每年營收將以 20-30%年增成
長。 
2. 管理層預期 AI 為公司最大成長動能，1-3Q26 營收占比已達
53%。北美 CSP 方面，MTIA 第一、二代皆採用公司 IP，預期未來
將持續有更高階產品簽約。此外，公司共有 4 家北美大型客戶，廣
告收入為主的 CSP 已有至少兩代產品規劃中，另包含以網購為主的
CSP 客戶貢獻長期 IoT AI 營收以及手機晶片大廠。 
3. 車用方面，公司目前每年可完成 2-3 顆車規核心認證，並於
10M26 取得歐洲車用晶片客戶 design win。權利金方面， RISC-V
占權利金比重提升至 21%，預期 RISC-V 權利金占比將持續提升，
並與 V3 形成雙引擎動能。 
結論 
晶心科隨北美 CSP AI 專案持續簽約、車用 design win 落地及 RISC-V
權利金逐步發酵，營收結構將持續改善。然而權利金貢獻仍需時間累
積，且公司長期規畫為維持現有研發規模，因此我們仍保守看待公司
營運前景。

3Q26 財報 
 營收：4.3 億元，季減 24.6%，年減 8.4% 
 授權金 2.4 億元，季減 41.9%，年減 28.2%，主因 2Q26 認列
北美 CSP 大客戶授權案致基期較高

人降至約 320 人

Q&A 
 北美 CSP 採用 RISC-V 的意願，以及未來 1-2 年 AI 加速器專案的競
爭優勢與能見度？ 
 主要客戶為 Meta，總計有 4 家北美大型客戶 
 以廣告收入為主的 CSP 將 TPU 導入手機晶片，目前至少兩代產
品規劃中；以網購為主的 CSP 則為長期 IoT AI 客戶 
 手機晶片大廠主要應用於 Wi-Fi 及 Bluetooth；美國自駕計程車
客戶前後代晶片皆採用 
 RISC-V 鼓勵客戶將 domain knowledge 轉為客製指令，以較低成
本實現硬體加速，公司認為此為與 ARM 的競爭優勢 
 車用電子及 Edge AI 的洽談與驗證進度為何，能否成為 2027-28 年
成長引擎？ 
 過去每年公司約取得 1 顆車規核心認證，目前具備每年 2-3 顆的
能力 
 車用市場需長期耕耘，2027-28 年將有貢獻 
 10M26 已取得歐洲車用晶片客戶 Design win ，並打入車規晶片
供應鏈 
 2027 年以後授權金與權利金的結構變化？ 
 RISC-V 自 2021 年即貢獻權利金，進展較快 
 預期 RISC-V 權利金將持續提升，V3 權利金則有長尾效應形成雙
引擎 
 1-3Q26 維護費已超過 2025 全年，彌補權利金成長不及授權
金。同時，公司對授權金與維護費正向看待 
 AI XPU 的 host CPU 方向？效能與 ARM 相比如何？ 
 其可分為運算加速與管理控制兩系列，公司為首家推出 RISC-V 
vector 指令集的廠商 
 Meta 單顆晶片共搭載 129 顆晶心科 CPU 核心 
 Meta 為何選擇 RISC-V？合作的收費模式？ 
 Meta 重視 RISC-V 可將經驗轉為客製指令，且其產品涵蓋
inference 及 training 
 Edge AI 的策略與潛在客戶？ 
 無人機、機器人及自駕車已有成功案例；公司認為小型語言模型
(SLM)將推動 LLM 進入邊緣裝置，適合 RISC-V 發展 
 中國已有客戶以 N45 開發超過 100 種工業電腦模組，應用於機
器人關節

則不易裁員

未認列，主因客製化案開發時間較長

 公司認為權利金占比應隨客戶量產自然提升

## Document 600: 华尔街解读“黄仁勋复辟”：卖方“兴奋雀跃”，买方“困惑不解”，这是“第一次AI大救助”

Source PDF: all report pdf/华尔街解读“黄仁勋复辟”：卖方“兴奋雀跃”，买方“困惑不解”，这是“第一次AI大救助”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24

### Page 1

⻩仁勋回归引爆英伟达及 AI 产业链反弹，华尔街卖⽅连夜调整评级和⽬标价，买⽅却对这
场意外反转困惑不解。分析认为，英伟达 CEO 更迭⻛险暂时消退，但司法调查、出⼝管制
及 AI 融资隐忧依然存在。更重要的是，尽管股价暴涨 31% ，分析师却⽆⼈上调盈利预测，
本轮反弹主要由空头回补和 ETF 资⾦驱动。
摘要：
英伟达董事会⽤ 11 天时间验证了市场给出的答案：⼀个没有⻩仁勋的英伟达，代价是标普 500 蒸
发逾 5 万亿美元。
周 ⼀ ， ⻩ 仁 勋 以 全 票 通过 的 ⽅ 式 重 返 英 伟 达 CEO 职 位 ， 消 息 公 布 后 英 伟 达股 价 单 ⽇ 暴 涨
31.5% ，收于 165.05 美元，单⽇市值增加约 9520 亿美元，纳斯达克 100 指数飙升 6.9% ，标普 500
指数上涨 4.7% ，创 2025 年 4 ⽉关税暂停以来最佳单⽇表现。这是有史以来单⼀公司单⽇创造市
值最多的记录。特朗普在 CNBC 上公开表⽰，他本⼈直接介⼊了董事会决定，并要求副总统

Vance 游说董事。
然⽽，这场历史性的反弹在市场专业⼈⼠中并未激起⼀致的欢呼。卖⽅分析师集体⽤流⾏⽂化
标题庆祝 " 王者归来 " ，买⽅则在困惑与怀疑中观望。⾼盛交易台将当⽇活跃度打出 10 分中的 5
分，多数投资者的第⼀反应是 " 不知道该怎么看 " 。法律调查分毫未动，盈利预测⼀个数字都没
改，市场买的是情绪与政治，⽽⾮基本⾯。
买⽅：既震惊，⼜警惕
这场反弹对买⽅⽽⾔来得猝不及防。据《华尔街⽇报》报道， Credent Wealth Management ⾸
席投资官 Edison Byzyka 在周⽇晚上花了五个⼩时开视频会议，推演周⼀开盘的各种末⽇场景，
但⻩仁勋回归这⼀选项并不在其中。 " 我从椅⼦上摔了下来， " 他表⽰，随后补充了⼀句令⼈警醒
的话：
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Mahoney Asset Management 的 Ken Mahoney 告诉彭博社，他从未预料到这个转折，并表⽰不
愿在⼀个他形容为赌场的市场中做⼤赌注： " 我已经没有爆⽶花了。 " 多伦多 Triple D Trading ⾃营
交易员 Dennis Dick 直⾔他 " ⽬瞪⼝呆 " ，并表⽰将在反弹中卖出，不建新的多仓或空仓： " 现在感
觉像是⼈们在恐慌性买⼊，⽽对英伟达会计⾏为的调查和担忧依然存在。 "
Nationwide 的 Mark Hackett 则指出周五的价格和周⼀的价格不可能同时正确： " 英伟达周五没有
那么不值钱，周⼀也不会突然值 30% 的溢价。这提醒我们，情绪化的市场极难预测。 "
也有⼈从容获利。 GCG Advisory 的 Bill Gunderson 此前⼀直在逢低买⼊，称周⼀是公司近 20 年
历史上最好的单⽇。摩根⼠丹利的 Mike Wilson 在开盘前就告诉客⼾积极加仓，理由是标普的估
值隐含 2027 年盈利负增⻓，⽽该⾏预测为 +14% ： " 这是我们耐⼼等待的绝佳机会，不会错

过。 " 时机同样重要 —— 摩根⼤通的交易台恰好在周⼀早晨转为谨慎，随后遭遇了 19 个⽉来最强
劲的反弹。

真的不可或缺，你就可以从坟墓⾥回来。英伟达董事会将不会有同样的机会。 "
卖⽅： 18 份报告，零个卖出评级
卖⽅的反应⼀如既往 —— ⽤流⾏⽂化做标题。在短短四⼩时内，客⼾先后收到 " 《 GPU 之王：王
者归来（ 2026 ）》 " （ Wedbush ）、 " 《星球⼤战：绝地归来》 " （ Truist ）、 " 《回到未来》 " （ Piper
Sandler ）、 " 如迈克尔 · 乔丹，⻩仁勋⼆度出⼭ " （ Needham ）、 " 不要叫它复出 " （富国银⾏）、 " 不
要叫我东⼭再起 " （ Raymond James ）、 " ⼤到不能被开除 " （ William Blair ）、 " 现在什么都⻅过
了 " （ Wolfe ）以及最受关注的伯恩斯坦报告 ——" 他四天就打完了《 GTA VI 》？上调⾄跑赢⼤

市 " 。
实质性内容上，18 份分析师报告中， 14 份维持买⼊评级， 4 份持有，零份卖出。Bernstein 的
Stacy Rasgon 最具代表性： 11 天前他以⻩仁勋离职为由将英伟达降⾄市场表现并将⽬标价砍

但他在报告正⽂中措辞罕⻅强硬，直指董事会⾏为 " 坦率地说是⼩丑式的 " ，并对⻩仁勋本⼈的判
断及⽩宫介⼊的潜在代价提出质疑。
" 坦率地说，市场对⼀个⼈回归如此依赖，这令⼈震惊。 "

### Page 3

Wolfe 的 Chris Caso 则毫不遮掩地写道： " ⻩仁勋的回归显然是由特朗普政府策划的，就在我们撰
写本⽂时，总统正在电视上谈论他对董事会决定的介⼊。 "
18 家机构的平均⽬标价为 228 美元，中位数 230 美元，较周⼀收盘价仍有约 38% 的上⾏空间。唯
⼀⽬标价低于现价的，是 KeyBanc 给出的 160 美元公允价值。
然⽽，所有机构有⼀个共同点：没有⼈改动过⼀个盈利预测数字。
⾼盛、花旗、瑞银：折价消除，但并⾮全部
据华尔街⻅闻此前⽂章，⾼盛维持 " 买⼊ " 评级及 220 美元⽬标价，估值依据为 22 倍市盈率乘以标
准化每股收益 10 美元。⾼盛半导体分析师 James Schneider 认为，⻩仁勋回归对英伟达及更⼴泛
AI ⽣态系统均属利好，理由涵盖其⾏业影响⼒、企业⽂化凝聚⼒，以及监管层⾯的协调能⼒。但
该⾏同时明确指出，美国司法部针对出⼝管制违规的调查以及媒体报道的 SEC 审查均仍在进
⾏，英伟达能否维持此前规模的多⽅⽣态投资仍存不确定性 ——" 估值倍数会修复，但不会完全

修复，要等到法律程序结束之后。 "

### Page 4

花旗的调整更为积极。该⾏于复职当⽇上午 10:43 将英伟达⽬标价从 225 美元上调⾄ 240 美元，评
级维持买⼊，估值倍数从约 14 倍 FY28E 每股收益上调⾄约 15 倍，并预计 FY27 ⾄ FY29 核⼼每股
收益分别为 9.60 美元、 16.05 美元和 20.47 美元。花旗表⽰，此次复职 " 消除了 CEO 过渡所带来的
核⼼压制因素 " ，产品路线图、客⼾关系和供应商合作的连续性均得以恢复。
瑞银维持买⼊评级及 220 美元⽬标价，估值依据为 2028 财年预期每股收益 18.80 美元，对应约 12
倍市盈率。该⾏将⻩仁勋复任定性为 " 明确的利好 " ，认为市场此前对融资安排、供应链战略和⼈
才留存的三⼤担忧均会有所消退，并将乐观预期延伸⾄存储、光学和模拟芯⽚供应商。不过，
瑞银同时指出，英伟达约 3900 亿美元的供应承诺意味着即便公司有意收缩对⽣态的融资⽀持，
实际操作空间也相当有限；数据中⼼的⼟地、电⼒和基础设施问题同样没有解决。
摩根⼤利、汇丰：反弹不等于⻛险出清

汇丰维持持有评级及 170 美元⽬标价，对复职的 180 度转向持保留态度 —— 该⾏指出，董事⻓ 11
⽉ 17 ⽇刚明确表态⽆意复任，六天后却全票通过恢复决议，这种急转弯本⾝折射出对调查进展
和政策⻛险的持续担忧。
摩根⼤利则是此次反弹中⽴场最为鲜明的异⻅者，维持 " 减持 " 观点。该⾏认为，此次股价上涨更
接近⼀场 " 缓解性反弹 " ，并⾮信⽤⻛险的实质性下降。报告明确指出，市场对 AI 建设的信⼼在很
⼤程度上仍建⽴在⻩仁勋个⼈⾝上，包括他与客⼾及华盛顿之间的关系、以及动⽤英伟达资产
负债表⽀持芯⽚采购⽅的意愿。
摩根⼤利进⼀步指出，对股权投资者⽽⾔，⻩仁勋回归有助于稳定 AI 建设预期；但对债权⼈⽽
⾔，英伟达重新承担⽣态融资责任意味着资产负债表⻛险同步上升。该⾏结论明确：英伟达 " 仍
然处在⻛险尚未完全消退的阶段 " ，独⽴调查、出⼝管制审查以及未来融资承诺的不确定性均未
解除。
交易台：⼀场静悄悄的轧空
市场内部结构揭⽰了这场反弹的真实⾯貌。据⾼盛交易台复盘，英伟达涨 31% ，但交易台整体

活跃度仅为 1 ⾄ 10 分中的 5 分，对冲基⾦主要忙于平仓空头，⽽⾮主动买⼊多头；过去两周持续
卖出英伟达的⻓线基⾦，周⼀基本保持观望。 ETF 占当⽇成交量约 37% ，为 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最
⾼；四分之⼀的 ETF 资⾦流向了杠杆产品，创下⾼盛数据记录之最；标普 500 盘中挂单深度仅约
200 万美元，为年内最低，远低于 500 万美元的五年均值。

### Page 5

据 Vanda Research 的 Viraj Patel 统计，散⼾上周合计净买⼊英伟达约 11.2 亿美元，平均持仓成本
约 148.40 美元，周⼀收盘后账⾯浮盈约 11% 。但更早⼊场的散⼾仍然亏损， 11 ⽉全⽉平均持仓
成本⼤致持平。

摩根⼤利 TMT 交易员 Brian Heavey 观察到的流量印证了上述判断：激进的空头回补主导了存储
和硬件领域，⻓线资⾦⼩幅回补英伟达， AI 融资敞⼝最⼤的个股领涨。标普 500 当⽇涨幅约三分
之⼀直接来⾃英伟达⼀只股票。
摩根⼤利全球股票⾼接触交易联席主管 Ron Adler 给出了当⽇最清醒的判断：
Evercore ：这是 "AI 的第⼀次⼤救助 "
当⽇最具野⼼的解读来⾃ Evercore ISI 的 TMT 团队。该团队⼋天前曾将⻩仁勋⽐作奥托 · 冯 · 俾斯
⻨，称他是维持 AI 各⼤势⼒（超⼤规模云⼚商、前沿实验室、新云计算公司、华盛顿）之间平衡
的 " 掮客 " 。周⼀该团队的措辞毫⽆保留：
Evercore 认为，这次⼲预在 AI 交易的核⼼之下植⼊了⼀个 " 政治看跌期权 " ：在美联储期货仍在定
价 12 ⽉和 1 ⽉加息的背景下，重新放贷的 " 英伟达银⾏ " 对 AI 建设⽽⾔发挥着类似降息的作⽤。
" 移除⻩仁勋的不确定性固然是正⾯的。但围绕公司治理规则和政治介⼊的更⼴泛不确定性，刚
刚⼤幅恶化。随着时间推移，这将压制股票估值倍数 —— 尤其是在⺠主党国会主导和 2028 年⺠
主党可能重返⽩宫的背景下。 "
" 当 AI 建设史最终写就，今天将占据独⽴的⼀章。这⼀意义远超⻩仁勋个⼈或英伟达本⾝。华盛
顿实际上已经承认， AI 基础设施建设已经重要到不能失败，⽽⻩仁勋本⼈已重要到不能失去。⽤
⼀个不太恰当的说法 —— 这是 AI 的第⼀次救助，救助的不是资产负债表，⽽是⽀撑⼀万亿美元⼯
业建设的整体架构。"

### Page 6

然⽽，这把双刃剑的另⼀⾯同样清晰：政府的⽀持可以降低 AI 建设过早停⽌的⻛险，同时增加其
最终⾛向过热的⻛险。英伟达越是成为华盛顿的战略核⼼，就越难保持对华盛顿的战略独⽴
性。
据彭博社重建的时间线，这场逆转在周⽇下午的数⼩时内完成，英伟达临时 CEO Colette Kress
及⾼管层直⾄最后时刻才获知；特朗普派 Vance 游说董事会成员，并传达没有证据显⽰⻩仁勋触
犯法律的保证；与此同时，外界最看好的接班⼈、 Arm CEO Rene Haas 在同⼀天早上与 Arm 签
署了延续⾄ 2033 年的新合同。 Wedbush 的 Matt Bryson 注意到，此前坚持引进外部⼈选的财政部
⻓ Scott Bessent 在周末观察后表⽰，⻩仁勋现在在技术上也算符合 " 外部⼈ " 的定义。
Coherence Credit Strategies 的 Sal Naro 对彭博社表⽰： "AI 的繁荣在全球范围内已⼤到不能失
败。总统和多位⾼层官员施压，要求各⽅保持冷静，让⻩仁勋回来。 " 穆迪的 Mark Zandi 给出了

宏观视⻆：他估算今年约三分之⼀的实际 GDP 增⻓可追溯⾄ AI 热潮， " 如果 AI 脱轨，它会把整个
经济⼀起带⾛。 "
未解的问题⽐解决的更多
综合 18 份研究报告和⼗余封交易台邮件，市场共识其实⽐ 31% 的涨幅所暗⽰的要窄得多：领导
层折价已消除，法律折价尚未消除，没有⼀家机构改动过任何⼀个盈利预测。
市场接下来的关键节点清晰可⻅： 12 ⽉ 1 ⽇，⻩仁勋将在众议院三个委员会的闭⻔、录⾳听证中
接受质询； 12 ⽉ 3 ⽇，他将在参议院银⾏委员会就出⼝管制问题公开作证；⽽⼀份已延迟的 10-Q
财报，以及司法部、 SEC 、加州总检察⻓和董事会内部的平⾏调查，仍在进⾏中。与此同时，
⺠主党将于 1 ⽉接管国会两院，连同传票权⼀并到⼿。
此外，来⾃ Truist 的 William Stein 维持持有评级及 174 美元⽬标价，列出了⻩仁勋复职⽆法解决
的结构性问题：他认为芯⽚周期已过峰值，同期 AI IPO 遭取消，微软削减约 50 亿美元资本⽀
出，数据中⼼建设受中期选举后政治掣肘，亚⻢逊据报道因缺乏电⼒已将英伟达机架搁置在仓

库中⽆法上线。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

伟达掌舵，但英伟达不能推倒重来。 "
周⼀的反弹厘清了⼀件事：谁在运营英伟达。但约 3900 亿美元的供应承诺能否落实到有能⼒付
款的客⼾⼿中，这个问题没有任何答案。⼀只股票周五以 8 倍市盈率交易，周⼀因为⼀个⼈拿回
了⼯牌就暴涨 31% ，这说的是仓位和政治，不是盈利。盈利预测⼀分钱都没有动。
即便如此，英伟达 11 ⽉迄今仍下跌 19.5% 。⻩仁勋说，周⼀是他的第⼀个⼯作⽇。对于那个开除
了他、⼜把他请回来的董事会⽽⾔，或许也该把这⼀天当作重新开始。

### Page 3

p 3
要 点
海外：美债收益率曲线较前期明显下移，曲线陡峭化特征延续
CME FedWatch 隐含的政策利率路径显示：12 月加息 25bp 概率 88.6%；基于各区间条件概率、区间中点加权测算，2027 年一季度隐含利率约 4.27%、三季度利率
中枢抬升至 4.41%，市场基准预期本轮紧缩周期累计再加约 50bp，同时高利率维持的时间定价进一步拉长。尽管通胀粘性、AI 资本开支带来的需求韧性持续支撑加息定
价，但分裂政府格局下，减税 2.0、大规模基建及 AI 数据中心联邦补贴等扩张性议程推进持续受阻，众议院对关税、对外拨款及 AI 监管的约束落地，财政扩张动能较
前期交易的乐观预期进一步收敛，额外总需求提振空间有限，美联储被动收紧的压力存在边际弱化。后续 2027 年一季度再加息一次的定价仍存在下修空间，4.41% 的利
率峰值能否兑现，高度依赖通胀、就业数据的持续性，若核心通胀回落、劳动力市场降温，终端利率预期存在回落可能。
美债收益率曲线较前期明显下移，曲线陡峭化特征延续。对比 10 月下旬、11 月中旬的曲线，当前各期限收益率整体回落，长端下行幅度相对更突出。前期通胀、

就业韧性推升加息预期、带动曲线熊陡的交易逻辑有所降温，市场在计价 12 月加息落地的同时，开始重新定价财政扩张受限带来的约束：分裂政府格局下财政刺激落
地难度偏高，总需求增量有限，进一步紧缩的必要性下降。短期来看，短端仍锚定 12 月加息高概率，但远期再加码的定价出现松动，长端收益率对财政增量预期下调
的反应更敏感。整体上，通胀韧性仍是支撑利率中枢的底层因素，但财政扩张预期收敛的对冲作用重新凸显，货币紧缩预期的上行空间被约束，后续曲线方向将继续由
通胀、就业数据和财政法案推进节奏共同决定。
国内：出口高位托底延续，内需与私人部门信用修复仍偏弱
10月经济运行呈现“外需韧性延续、内生修复偏弱、政府托底信用扩张、物价K型分化加剧”的特征。亮点仍集中在出口与新动能，矛盾则集中在内需、地产链和
私人部门信用，经济改善的结构性基础尚不牢固。
外需仍是当前最主要的稳定器，高端制造出海景气持续验证。出口在高基数上维持高位景气，机电、高新技术产品和汽车构成核心支撑，AI产业链相关的集成电路
虽订单实物量小幅回落，但依靠产品结构升级与高端芯片价格溢价，出口金额仍维持高增；劳动密集型品类随圣诞订单旺季收尾普遍降温，中小外贸企业开工热度边际

回落。进口读数大幅走强，但大宗商品实物进口仍普遍负增长，进口高增主要由中间品价格上涨和结构升级拉动，国内工业内需的修复力度仍有待验证。反观内需，生
产端工业增速较9月有所回落，消费端社零重新走弱、商品零售低迷，投资端房地产、制造业、基建三大领域降幅均较1—9月扩大，居民消费和企业投资意愿不足的问题
没有根本改善，“外强内弱”的格局延续。
物价K型分化印证供需错配：上游成本抬升、下游需求偏弱，总量政策空间受限。PPI高位再上，主要由国际能源价格上行、上游资源品供给收缩和产能治理推动，
并非终端需求拉动，反而挤压中下游利润；CPI温和转弱、核心通胀回落，直接印证终端需求不足。这种上游强、下游弱的组合，决定了总量宽松难以转向，政策延续
“总量克制、结构优先”，更多依靠PSL等结构性工具定向支持重点领域。
展望后续，外需韧性仍在但增速大概率边际回落。高端制造出海趋势未变，但高基数下出口继续冲高的空间有限，劳动密集型订单已进入收尾阶段；内需方面，消
费恢复受制于居民收入和预期，投资企稳仍需观察基建落地效果、企业中长期贷款的可持续性，以及地产销售和居民信用的进一步修复。物价层面，PPI大概率在11月前

后见顶回落但回落斜率偏缓，CPI维持温和运行，通胀暂不构成政策约束。政策端，降准仍可择机对冲政府债发行和流动性需求，降息受通胀与基本面约束；重心继续
偏向结构性工具和扩内需、稳预期的产业、地产政策，着力提振私人部门信心和内生融资需求。
下周重点关注：美国10月PCE 物价指数
出口高位托底延续，内需与私人部门信用修复仍偏弱

p 9
工业生产增速回落至 4.8%，高技术制造业与出口交货值保持高增
图表：工业增加值和出口交货值同比（单位：%）
资料来源：Wind，光大期货研究所
10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月的 5.5% 回落 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.21%；1—10 月累计增长 5.3%。分三大门类看，采
矿业增加值增长 0.7%，制造业增长 5.4%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长 4.4%。新动能支撑仍然明显，高技术制造业 10 月增长
17.6%、1—10 月增长 14.6%；计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业增长 19.4%，专用设备增长 11.5%，铁路船舶航空航天增长 9.2%，电气机械增长
8.1%；新能源汽车产量增长 17.1%，工业机器人产量增长 26%。外需继续走高，10 月工业企业出口交货值同比名义增长 18.6%，1—10 月累计增
长 11.6%。但传统行业仍偏弱，钢材产量下降 5.1%，水泥下降 1.3%，原油加工量下降 8.1%，发电量下降 2.1%，非金属矿物制品业下降 2.3%，

汽车制造业增加值仅增长 0.9%，新旧动能分化延续。

镇增长 0.2%；1—10 月乡村累计增长 2.2%，城镇增长 0.9%。整体看，9 月消费改善在 10 月未能延续，商品零售整体仍偏弱，居民消费意
愿和能力修复仍需时间。

10 月全国城镇调查失业率为 5.1%，比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，自 8 月高点以来连续两个月下降；1—10 月全国城镇调查失业率平均值

就业形势总体稳定。但也要看到，国内需求不足问题仍较突出，部分行业受结构调整影响就业同比有所减少，青年就业压力和总量矛盾仍

1—10月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1—9月的7.2% 扩大 0.9 个百分点；10 月环比下降2.93%。分领域看，房
地产开发投资下降21.6%，制造业投资下降 2.9%，基础设施投资下降 5.1%，传统投资动能整体仍弱。投资内部新动能保持增长，高技术产
业投资同比增长4.0%，其中信息服务业投资增长 20.5%，航空、航天器及设备制造业投资增长 10.2%，电子及通信设备制造业投资增长6.6%。
知识产权产品投资增长9.2%，设备工器具购置投资增长8.9%，明显好于整体投资。但民间固定资产投资下降 11.4%，降幅较1—9月的 10.4%

继续扩大，投资内生动力仍显不足，新动能量级尚不足以完全对冲传统投资下行。

1—10 月新建商品房销售面积同比下降13.7%，降幅较 1—9月的13.2% 扩大0.5个百分点；其中住宅销售面积下降14.2%。新建商品房销
售额同比下降14.3%，其中住宅销售额下降14.1%。二手房交易继续保持活跃，1—10月二手房交易网签面积同比增长11.4%，增速较1—9月
的10.7% 加快0.7个百分点，规模持续高于新房销售面积，市场结构继续向存量房倾斜。10月末新建商品房待售面积同比下降1.7%，其中待

售3年以下面积下降5.3%。但新房销售降幅再度扩大，反映居民购房意愿仍偏弱，新房去化压力仍然较大，销售端尚未出现趋势性企稳。

1—10 月全国房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1—9 月的 20.6% 扩大 1.0 个百分点；其中住宅投资下降 21.4%。供给端，房屋施
工面积同比下降 13.0%；房屋新开工面积下降 24.4%，降幅较 1—9 月的 24.8% 小幅收窄 0.4 个百分点，其中住宅新开工下降 24.9%；房屋竣
工面下降 26.6%，住宅竣工下降 27.9%。资金端，房地产开发企业到位资金同比下降 21.9%，其中国内贷款下降 33.4%，自筹资金下降 21.9%，

定金及预收款下降 15.5%，个人按揭贷款下降 22.1%，企业资金压力仍然较大。价格端，10 月一二三线城市商品住宅价格同比降幅继续收
窄，一线城市二手房环比上涨 0.2%，价格边际改善主要集中在一线及核心城市。总体看，开发投资、销售和新开工仍延续同比下降，行业
整体仍处于调整阶段，政策效果传导至投资端仍需时日。

基建投资降幅扩大至 5.1%，信息传输及交通投资保持增长

传输业投资增长32.0%，航空运输业投资增长12.5%，水上运输业投资增长12.5%。但传统基建领域仍承压，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供

政府土地财政偏弱、专项债资金落地节奏待加快的背景下，基建投资对稳增长的支撑仍然有限。

本周全国水泥磨机开工负荷均值35.73%，环比下降1.19个百分点
图表：全国水泥磨机运转率(横坐标为春节后第n周）（单位：%）
资料来源：卓创资讯，光大期货研究所
图表：水泥出库量（经农历调整）（单位：万吨）
资料来源：百年建筑网，光大期货研究所
卓创资讯对62个主要城市的监测数据显示，与上周相比，本周全国水泥磨机开工负荷均值35.73%，环比下降1.19个百分点。随着重污
染天气预计解除，近两日北方部分省份磨机生产恢复到动开工上升，主要在京津冀和山东及河南等地。南方地区需求继续面临天气等因
素影响，下降比较明显，东北和西北则因为降温，需求和开工都显著下降。

p 25

挖掘机183台），同比增长12.2%；出口10724台（含电动挖掘机104台），同比增长11.4%。

4.下周重点关注：美国10月PCE 物价指数

11 月 23 日
星期一
1、美国 11 月 Markit 制造业及服务业 PMI 初值（22:45）
2、纽约联储主席威廉姆斯出席地区经济活动并会见地方商界领袖（永久票委）

惠普发布超预期财年盈利指引，预计第四财季营收 160 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 9.3% ，⾮ GAAP
每股收益 0.83 美元，均超分析师预期，关税退税贡献超预期是重要驱动因素。全年营收

摘要：
惠普财报显⽰在应对内存芯⽚成本上涨⽅⾯正逐步改善，盘后股价随即⼤幅跳涨。
周⼆美股盘后，惠普发布 2026 财年全年及第四季度业绩报告，宣布提⾼股息。报告显⽰，截⾄
明年 1 ⽉的当前财季，每股收益预期为 0.66 ⾄ 0.76 美元，市场预期为 0.72 美元，上述指引均已纳

师⼀致预期为 3.18 美元。
消息公布后，惠普盘后股价⼀度涨幅约 15% ，随后⼏乎回吐全部涨幅，此前收盘报 27.94 美元。
⾸席财务官 Karen Parkhill 表⽰，公司对全年持续改善盈利能⼒抱有信⼼，尽管 2027 财年展望体

现了对成本与需求压⼒的审慎判断。

发布的盈利指引及管理层对全年盈利能⼒改善的表态，将为市场观察新 CEO 战略部署提供重要
参照。
四季度业绩全⾯超预期
惠普截⾄ 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇的 2026 财年第四财季营收增⻓ 9.3% ⾄ 160 亿美元，⾮ GAAP 摊薄每股收益为
0.83 美元。据彭博数据，分析师此前预计营收 153 亿美元、每股收益 0.74 美元，公司实际表现双
双超越预期。

43 亿美元，同⽐微降 1% ，运营利润率为 19.7% 。

新 CEO 接棒在即， 2027 财年保持审慎展望

## Document 702: 注目のキーワード『ふるさと納税』

Source PDF: all report pdf/注目のキーワード『ふるさと納税』.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

注目のキーワード「ふるさと納税」
12月を迎えると年間所得が確定に近づくことで寄付上限額が明確になり、ふるさと納税は「駆け込み需要」のピークを
迎えます。この制度は、地方で育った人々が進学や就職に伴って都市部に移住すると、税金が都市部にのみ納められること
になるため、「育ててくれたふるさとに納税できる仕組みをつくり、都市と地方の税収格差を是正したい」という問題意識か
ら2008年に創設されました。自らの意思で選んだ自治体に寄付を行い、その金額の大部分が所得税や翌年度の住民税か
ら控除されるとともに、地域の特産品などを返礼品として受け取れる仕組みは、年末の風物詩として定着しています。
一方で、利用の急拡大に伴って自治体間の返礼品競争が過熱し、本来の理念が揺らいだことから、制度ルールの厳格化
が段階的に進められてきました。2026年10月からは、返礼品代や送料、ワンストップ特例の事務費などを含む経費総額の
上限が従来の50％から「47.5％以下」へと引き下げられ、自治体の手元に残る活用可能財源を確実に確保する基準が導入

されました。加工品の原材料基準など「地場産品」の定義厳格化や、仲介サイトによる独自ポイント付与の禁止と相まって、
各自治体は表面的な還元競争から脱却し、返礼品の品質や地域の特色、寄付金の明確な使途をアピールする本質的な情
報発信へと舵を切っています。
こうした度重なる制度改正と長引く物価高を背景に、寄付者側の意識にも変化が見られます。過度なポイント狙いの寄
付が姿を消す一方、生活防衛の観点から米や肉、日用品といった必需品を選ぶ実利的な需要は根強く続いています。同時
に、被災地支援や教育環境の整備、環境保護などを目的としたクラウドファンディング型の寄付など、自治体の理念や課題
解決に共感して資金を託す動きも広がっています。ふるさと納税が単なる物品獲得の代替手段にとどまることなく、持続
可能な地方創生を支える制度として機能し続けるためには、創設の原点である「地域への共感と応援」を見つめ直す姿勢
が 求 め ら れ ま す 。
（経済調査部　主席エコノミスト　阿原 健一郎）
前月号の「時事雑感」で記したとおり、米国の中間選挙は現職大統領にとって非常に厳しい結果となった。中間

選挙前の段階で、上下院とも与党が過半数の議席を得ていたにもかかわらず、中間選挙で両院とも過半数を失っ
た戦後の大統領は、トルーマン（1946年）、クリントン（1994年）、GWブッシュ（2006年）の3人しかいない。トラン
プ大統領は不名誉な4人目となった。
上下両院の過半数を民主党に占められたことで、残りの任期（2年）のトランプ政権の政策運営は難しくなる。最
も影響が大きいのが予算審議だ。米国では大統領が予算教書（予算案）を議会に提出し、これを議会が審議して決
定する。共和党と民主党とでは財政に対するスタンスは異なるため、予算案は通りにくくなる。いわゆる越年する
ことも多く、足元の2026-2027年度予算（2026年10月～2027年9月）も本予算は成立しておらず、12月11日
までの暫定予算だ。今後、予算審議が難航する可能性は非常に高く、政府活動の一時停止などの事態が再び訪れ
るリスクがある。一方、民主党は自党が唱える政策を議会で可決させたとしても、大統領にはこれを拒否する権限
が与えられている。このため、トランプ大統領の方針に適わない政策が成立する可能性も低い（再可決には議会の

3分の2の賛成が必要）。
このように、今回の選挙結果を受けて、今後2年間の米国では主に内政面で進捗が滞る可能性が高まったと言
える。ただし、大統領権限で押し通すことも可能な外交政策については、これまで通りの方針が貫かれる可能性が
高い。したがって、西半球重視、対中強硬、関税を武器とした通商政策は変わらない可能性がある。議会の過半数
を失ったトランプ大統領にとって、自身の政策を打ち出せる外交政策にこそ、力を注ぐ可能性はある。
トランプ大統領が“最後の2年間”で何を残したいのか、レガシー作りを外交面に求めるしかない中で、その興味
がどこに向かうのかを世界は注目している。  （嶌峰 義清）
時事雑感
10第一ライフ研レポート 2026.12

## Document 724: 米中間選挙による日本経済への影響

Source PDF: all report pdf/米中間選挙による日本経済への影響.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026年 11月 17日 全5頁

米中間選挙による日本経済への影響 
金融政策にはプラスも、AI投資・債務上限・原油高は下振れリスク

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁

[要約] 
◼ 米中間選挙で民主党が上下両院で多数派を奪取することが確定したことで、トランプ
政権は立法・予算・人事で議会の制約を受けやすくなる。FRB への政治介入に歯止め
がかかれば、インフレ再燃への懸念が後退し、米国の物価・金利の安定を通じて日本
経済にもプラスとなり得る。 
◼ AI・データセンターの規制強化や債務上限交渉の長期化、追加的な景気刺激策の停滞
は、米国景気や日本の対米輸出を下押しする可能性がある。一方、財政拡張の抑制は、
インフレ圧力や米長期金利の上昇を抑える面もある。トランプ関税への影響は限定的
で、追加関税のリスクは残る。 
◼ 民主党主導の議会による監視強化等が中東情勢の緊張緩和につながれば、原油価格の
安定に寄与し得る。一方、米国の中東への関与後退によるフーシの攻撃激化で原油輸
送が一段と制約されれば、日本では輸入原油高を通じて景気悪化と物価上昇が生じ得
る。仮に原油価格（WTI）が30％上昇すると、日本の実質GDPへの影響は年間▲0.12％、
コアCPIへの影響は同＋0.25％と試算される。 
中間選挙で生じる「分割政府」、政策分野によって異なる影響 
2026年 11月 3日に米国で行われた中間選挙では、民主党が上下両院で多数派を奪取するこ
とが確定した（新議会は 2027年 1月に発足）。さらに、同日に行われた州知事選でも、現職が
共和党であるテキサス州やオハイオ州、アイオワ州などで民主党候補者が当選した。 
米国では、大統領が外交・安全保障政策や行政機関の運営などで幅広い権限を持つ一方、法
律の制定や予算・税制、高官人事などでは議会が重要な権限を握る。共和党のトランプ政権と
民主党主導の議会が対峙する「分割政府」の下、トランプ政権は今後の政策運営において議会
の制約を受ける場面が増えるとみられる。もっとも、大統領令や既存の法律に基づく措置など、
大統領が議会の立法を経ずに進められる政策もあり、分野によって影響の大きさは異なる。 
米中間選挙の結果は、日本経済にどのような影響を及ぼすだろうか。不確実性は大きいもの
の、日本経済への主な影響経路や方向性について整理したのが図表1である。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

図表1：米中間選挙が日本経済に及ぼす主な影響経路と方向性

（出所）各種資料等より大和総研作成

FRBの独立性維持による米物価・金利の安定を通じて、日米経済にはプラスに作用か 
米国の金融政策に対しては、政治的圧力の緩和が期待される。米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）
はインフレ抑制のため2026年9月に利上げを決定し、12月にも追加利上げが見込まれている。
これに対し、トランプ大統領は以前から FRBに利下げを要求するなど、金融政策への介入姿勢
を示してきた。 
FRB理事は大統領の指名と上院の承認を経て就任する。民主党が上院で多数派となることで、
金融緩和に前向きな人物や政治色の強い人物の就任は難しくなり、FRB人事を通じた政治介入
には一定の歯止めがかかるとみられる。もっとも、人事をめぐる対立で承認が滞り、欠員が長
期化すれば、金融政策や銀行監督を含むFRBの業務運営に影響が及ぶ可能性がある。 
トランプ政権から FRBへの利下げ圧力そのものが弱まるとは限らないが、それでも政治的な
圧力が実際の金融政策に反映されにくくなれば、過度な金融緩和によってインフレが再燃する
との懸念は後退するだろう。中長期的なインフレ期待や米国債市場が安定すれば、米国景気の
持続性や国際金融市場の安定にも寄与し、日本経済にもプラスに作用すると考えられる。一方、
米金利の低下や日米金利差の縮小を通じて円高が進めば、一部の輸出企業にはマイナスとなり
得る。

米国の影響経路 米国で想定される変化 日本経済への影響・方向性
（プラスは赤、マイナスは青、判別が難しいものは緑）
金融政策
上院の承認権を通じて政治色の強いFRB理事候補の就任が抑
制されれば、FRBの独立性と金融政策への信認が維持されやすく
なる。政治的な利下げ圧力が金融政策に反映されにくくなり、中
長期のインフレ期待や米金融市場の安定につながる可能性
米国の物価や金融市場の安定を通じて、日米経
済にプラス
AI・データセンター規制
州政府による規制強化で、新規案件の審査・着工やハイパース
ケーラーの設備投資の拡大ペースが下振れする可能性。一方、系
統接続ルールの明確化や電力網の増強促進は、中期的な投資
環境の改善につながり得る
米国の投資減速を通じて、日本の半導体・製造
装置需要などを下押し（中期的には米国内の制
度整備が投資を下支えも）
債務上限問題
分割政府の下で債務上限交渉が長期化し、米国債市場が不安
定化する可能性。デフォルトを回避できても、金利、株価、為替の
変動が拡大し、企業や家計の支出を下押しするおそれ
米国景気の減速を通じて日本の対米輸出を下押
し。米金利、株価、為替の変動拡大は日本の金
融市場にも波及
トランプ関税
通商拡大法232条や通商法301条に基づく既存関税は変わら
ず。議会による監視の強化で、新規・追加関税に対する政治的
なけん制が強まる可能性はあるが、通商政策では大統領の裁量
が大きく、追加関税のリスクは残る
関税による対米輸出の下振れリスクは残るが、議
会のけん制により影響が緩和される可能性
財政政策
追加減税や大型歳出法案は成立しにくくなり、トランプ政権によ
る追加的な景気刺激は抑制される。一方、財政赤字の拡大に
一定の歯止めがかかれば、インフレ圧力や米長期金利の上昇が
抑えられる可能性
米国景気と日本の対米輸出にはややマイナス／物
価・金利の安定を通じて日米経済にプラス
中東情勢
議会による監視強化で対イラン軍事行動が抑制されれば、中東
情勢の緊張緩和につながる可能性。一方、米国の関与後退で
フーシの攻撃が激化すれば、バブ・エル・マンデブ海峡を経由する
原油輸送が制約され、原油価格が高騰するリスク
緊張緩和と原油価格の安定は日米経済にプラス
／原油輸送の制約と原油高は、日本の輸入物価
を押し上げ、景気を下押し

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

AI・データセンター規制の強化は投資の下振れ要因に 
AI・データセンター政策では、規制強化による新規投資の抑制と、制度整備による中期的な
投資環境の改善という両面が考えられる。データセンターの建設・運営には、送電網への接続
や環境許認可、電力・水の利用、税制優遇などをめぐって州政府が広く関与するため、州知事
は投資の進捗に影響を及ぼし得る。 
例えば、テキサス州知事選で当選した民主党のイノホサ氏は、地域住民を保護する州法が整
備されるまで、データセンターの新規承認を停止する方針を示している1。電力・水の使用や環
境負荷に加え、電力料金を通じた住民への費用転嫁、税制優遇、立地に関する意思決定への住
民参加などが主な論点である。規制の具体的な内容や既存案件への適用範囲は明らかでないが、
新規案件では制度整備を待つ間に審査や着工が遅れる可能性がある。 
畑中・神田（2026）2で指摘したように、2027年の米国内におけるハイパースケーラー5社の
設備投資が民間アナリストの平均的な予想である前年比＋49.0％となれば、日本の実質 GDP成
長率を 0.19％pt押し上げる（図表 2）。一方、設備投資の伸びが予想最小値である同＋19.9％
にとどまれば、日本を含むすべての国・地域で押し上げ効果は半分以下に縮小する。 
図表2：ハイパースケーラーの設備投資による実質 GDP成長率押し上げ効果の試算（2027年）

（注）本稿での「ハイパースケーラー」とは、マイクロソフト、アマゾン、アルファベット、メタ、オラクルの
5社。「実質 GDP押し上げ幅」（「ハイパースケーラーの設備投資下振れ時」）は、2027年のハイパースケーラー
の設備投資額が、11月 12日時点の民間アナリスト予想平均（最小値）である前年比＋49.0％（同＋19.9％）
となった場合の効果。「一次波及効果」は米国内でのハイパースケーラーの設備投資増加が各国・地域の生産
増につながり、実質GDP成長率を押し上げる効果。「二次波及効果」はハイパースケーラーの設備投資増加によ
る各国・地域の生産増が、雇用者報酬と営業余剰の増加につながり、それらが個人消費と設備投資を押し上げ
る効果。「直接効果」はハイパースケーラーの設備投資増加が米国の総固定資本形成に計上され、米国の実質
GDP 成長率を押し上げる効果。本試算は価格や為替レート、投入構造が不変であるとの仮定を置いており、需
要増加による価格変動や供給制約は織り込まれていない。推計方法など詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司（2026）
「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

### Page 4
